# Decisions (autopilot)

One line per fork: `D-nn · chose X over Y · why · reversible?`

- D-01 · Mojo calls AppKit/UIKit through the Objective-C runtime, over a SwiftUI skin or GPU-drawn UI · the owner picked "Mojo drives Apple UI" · yes
- D-02 · look up `objc_msgSend` at runtime (`OwnedDLHandle`) over `external_call` · Mojo rejects one C symbol declared with two signatures in a module, and every UI call needs a different one · yes
- D-03 · pass CGRect/NSRect as four Float64 arguments over a C helper · on arm64 a 4-double struct travels in the same four float registers, and it keeps the app C-free; wrong on Intel, which is out of scope · yes
- D-04 · write the 3 Mojo runtime functions (AlignedAlloc, AlignedFree, GetStackTrace) in Mojo, in their own file, over linking Modular's runtime dylib or a C shim · app becomes self-contained and C-free; separate file because Mojo refuses to define them in a module that also calls them · yes
- D-05 · link on Linux with pixi's `ld64.lld` + hand-written `.tbd` symbol stubs over the real Apple SDK · the SDK needs an Apple login (out of bounds); the stubs only list names and install paths · yes
- D-06 · enter the app through the linker's `-e _mojo_entry` (LC_MAIN) over a C `main` · removes the last C file · yes
- D-07 · iOS uses the scene lifecycle (UIWindowScene) over a classic app-delegate window · recent iOS releases require scenes; it also avoids needing the screen size · yes
- D-08 · keep `ios-xtool/` (SwiftUI skin) as a fallback, marked superseded, over deleting it · reversible and costs nothing · yes
- D-09 · own signature verifier (`scripts/verify_signature.py`: recompute page + Info.plist hashes) over `rcodesign verify` · rcodesign says its verify is buggy and it fails on every ad-hoc signature; checking on the executable, not the bundle folder. Also tightened: entry point must be `_mojo_entry`, no stray bytes after the signature · yes
- D-10 · one variadic `msg()` that casts `objc_msgSend`'s address (copied from Mojo's own `_DLCallable`) over five `OwnedDLHandle` overloads · fewer lines, no dlopen/dlclose per call, nothing raises · yes
- D-11 · iPhone button uses Liquid Glass (`prominentGlassButtonConfiguration`, iOS 26+) when the class responds to it, else a filled capsule · phone follows the Liquid design language; the check keeps it working on iOS 17-18 · yes
- D-12 · reviewer findings: fixed 1-9 (protocol miss now printed; build.sh fails on Mojo errors and deletes stale objects; Gatekeeper advice for macOS 15+; BOOL encodings `B`; allowlist + stubs trimmed to exactly what's used; resource seal checked; iOS Dynamic Type; Cmd+W) · #10 (bundle ID clash) is a non-issue: xtool prefixes a per-user ID (ProvisioningIdentifiers.swift:24) · yes
- D-13 · mark binaries as built with SDK 26 (`-platform_version ios 17.0 26.0`, `macos 14.0 26.0`) over 17/14 · otherwise iOS/macOS 26 run the app in compatibility mode without the current look; unproven without the real SDK, one-line revert · yes

## Goal 2: declarative UI
- D-14 · view tree stored as a flat list of nodes (parent → child indexes) over a recursive struct · Mojo 1.1 rejects `List[View]` inside `View` ("non-Deinitable"); a flat list is also easy to diff and to pair with native handles · yes
- D-15 · Linux renderer: GTK4 + libadwaita over SDL3+Clay (ROADMAP decision 4) · the owner asked for *native* rendering; GTK is Linux's native toolkit, it's installed, and it can be run and screenshotted here · yes
- D-16 · platforms this run: Linux, Mac, iPhone; web/Android/Windows logged as next · only these have a Mojo build target and a native toolkit reachable today · yes
- D-17 · hosts create the app themselves (`App` requires `__init__()`) over passing an instance in · UIKit builds the window later in a scene callback that can't carry data · yes
- D-18 · Apple taps: button tag packs `(host address << 16) | node` over associated objects / ivars · no globals and no extra Objective-C runtime functions; checked at start-up that the address fits · yes
- D-19 · taps look the message up in the current tree by node index over storing the message on the widget · message changes (SET_MSG) then need no rewiring · yes
- D-20 · GTK test mode doesn't show the window (unless asked for a PNG) over always presenting it · checker runs don't pop windows on the owner's desktop; the widgets and taps are still real GTK · yes
- D-21 · screenshots rendered by GTK itself (widget → paintable → texture → PNG) over grabbing the screen with grim · no desktop disruption, exact pixels GTK drew · yes
- D-22 · stand-in for `KGEN_CompilerRT_fprintf` (writes the raw format string); allowlist + libSystem stubs gained `_bzero`, `_memmove`, `_memset`, `_strlen` (corrected after review: earlier this line said only bzero) · Mojo's bounds-check failure path needs fprintf; all four are real libSystem functions · yes
- D-23 · apps name the parameter `message`, not `msg`, over loosening the checker's platform-word list · `msg` is the Objective-C helper's name; the checker stays strict · yes
- D-24 · removed experiment 1's hand-written Mac/iPhone apps and `apple/build.sh` · superseded by the declarative hosts + `scripts/build.sh`; still in git history · yes (git)
- D-25 · review round 1 fixes: AppKit centre = 1 on arm64, row alignment CenterY = 10; node limit checked where tags are made (both Apple hosts); old controls deliberately not released (documented why); iOS content in the safe area with large buttons everywhere; goal 1's "no C/ObjC/Swift sources" check restored in M5 (I had dropped it unlogged); M2 now also shows the window and asserts GTK drew a PNG (a window flashes for a moment during `scripts/check`) · yes

## Goal 3: web, components, todo, Android, GPU
- D-26 · web host = FastHTML (Python) + HTMX, with the Mojo app compiled as a Python extension module (`PyInit_*` + `PythonModuleBuilder`) over Mojo-as-wasm or a Mojo HTTP server · owner's idea; Mojo has first-class Python modules; FastHTML gives HTML + the JS ecosystem · yes
- D-27 · plain `python-fasthtml` over fh-saas for the web host · it's a UI framework host, not a SaaS backend; fh-saas can sit on top later · yes
- D-28 · browser tests drive /usr/bin/chromium with Playwright over chrome-devtools-axi · the axi tool only looks for Google Chrome; Playwright + system Chromium needs no download and is scriptable from the checker · yes
- D-29 · GTK 'ignore our own changes' flag lives on the GApplication (g_object_set_data) over a Mojo field · signals re-enter while a `mut self` method runs; the compiler may drop a store only a re-entrant reader would see (found by tracing, see journal) · yes
- D-30 · GTK Toggle = GtkCheckButton (one widget, label included) over GtkSwitch + label · simplest native on/off with a label; reads naturally for todo items · yes
- D-31 · iOS dropdown = menu button with UIActions (handler = hand-built global block) over UIPickerView or a segmented control · it's the native iOS 15+ dropdown; blocks are the only way UIAction takes a handler · yes
- D-32 · GTK rebuilds deferred to the next idle turn (g_idle_add) over rebuilding inside the signal handler · the emitting widget was destroyed mid-signal (GTK CRITICALs); events are ignored until the new widgets are up · yes
- D-33 · GPU milestone before Android · GPU can be tested end to end here (2× RTX 3090); Android can't run here at all, so it gets the remaining time as an exploration · yes
- D-34 · review round (goal 3) fixes: web message lookup moved into Mojo (no hand-patched Python copy of the screen), per-tab lock, screen-shape version rejects stale clicks, typing + Return share one ordered HTMX queue (fixes the lost-task race the reviewer reproduced), htmx vendored + pinned (2.0.7), .sesskey untracked, focus kept on the text field across rebuilds (all hosts), Mac default button doesn't take Return when a field submits, todo message stride 1,000,000, JSON control chars escaped, 48px touch targets, 100dvh + safe area, reduced motion · yes
- D-35 · checker tightened after review (never loosened): goal 2's GTK hello + drawn-PNG checks restored in M1; any GTK CRITICAL, failed test step or browser page error fails; gallery checks every echo label + Return; todo run adds no-wait type-then-Return, Done filter and Clear done; app-file rule scans every existing app (M6 no longer waits on M8); images must be reported by both hosts · yes
- D-36 · Android tonight = a real arm64 libmojoui.so (Mojo app + core, ANativeActivity_onCreate, Android libs only) + a written plan, over a full APK · events need Java bytecode (listener interface) and packaging/testing needs Google's SDK licence + a device: both are the owner's call (docs/android.md) · yes
- D-37 · web bug found by the stricter gallery check: htmx inherits hx-vals, so the text input picked up its form's second=1 and every keystroke counted as Return; fixed by an explicit second=0, and the todo run now ends with a typed-but-not-submitted draft (would have caught it) · yes
- D-38 · review round 2 fixes: GPU helper checks `DeviceContext.number_of_devices()` before creating a context (no-GPU crashed instead of raising) and keeps one context (`Gpu` value); `Image` with the wrong pixel count becomes a blank picture of the stated size; web image route uses the real byte length; evicted web sessions are marked closed under their lock; focus without select-all (GTK) and on the next run-loop turn with the cursor at the end (Mac/iPhone); Android links only libc + liblog; doc claims corrected; checker adds a no-GPU run, NATIVE markers and the PNG test · yes
- D-39 · Image on Mac/iPhone via a PNG encoder written in Mojo (stored-deflate zlib + CRC) → NSImage/UIImage initWithData, over CoreGraphics bitmap calls · keeps the apps at exactly AppKit/UIKit + libobjc + libSystem; verified by decoding with Python's zlib · yes
- D-40 · review round 3 (code PASS): docs updated for Apple images; iPhone image view ≤ its pixel width with a fixed aspect ratio (preferred width at priority 750); images at least 1×1; PNG test checks blue too; GPU-per-tab concern measured and dismissed (shared context) · yes

## Goal 4: components real apps need, then a fancier app
- D-41 · froze last night's Apple builds in a git worktree (../mojo-ui-apple-test) for the owner's testing · the checker deletes and rebuilds build/, which would pull the rug out from under them · yes
- D-42 · htmx attribute inheritance switched off globally (htmx-config disableInheritance) after a second inheritance bug (list rows inherited the list's hx-target and silently sent nothing) · Encode Lessons in Structure: the same mistake can't recur · yes
- D-43 · web radio groups get unique names (choice in hx-vals): same-named radios across groups are one group to the browser, so picking a size unticked the tab and period (caught by M1) · yes
- D-44 · review (goal 4) fixes: GTK alert Escape = first button (close-response), web alert made modal without scripts (backdrop + focus + Escape key trigger) after showModal via hx-on::load didn't fire on out-of-band swaps, lazy-list trailing scroll + hx-sync replace + 204 on stale + rows re-sent after every update on all hosts, diff rebuilds when a Back button appears/goes or an alert's question changes, parse_cents overflow guard + $/commas + unit tests, 48px web targets, keyboard rows, iOS destructive style + alert dismissed when it leaves the view, list callbacks check the node kind; checker: Escape run, GTK alert really shown, web rows-in-page bound, expenses tests · yes
- D-45 · review round 2 (goal 4): GTK row refresh via a fresh model (items_changed with the same items is ignored by GTK and not ours to emit; reviewer proved it), proven by a 'Bump row 0' step reported as LISTROW on both hosts; web alert moved to an overlay beside an inert page (keyboard can't reach the page); alerts reported last on every host; iOS only treats its own UIAlertController as the alert (the compact date picker presents too); commas only as thousands separators; Space opens list rows · yes
- D-46 · review round 3 (PASS) should-fixes: GTK refresh splices fresh items over the range of rows drawn since the last refresh (not the whole 10,000-item model per keystroke); web alert overlay has its own id (n<i>-modal) and its own render function · yes

## Goal 5: layout as a thin layer
- D-47 · layout via flexbox-named settings mapped onto native layout, no engine of our own (owner's call, ROADMAP decision 10); sizes are border-box everywhere so numbers mean the same thing on every host · yes
- D-48 · web: grow = flex 1 1 0 (not flex-grow from content width, which wrapped rows) and Scroll lays out like a VStack (fill stretches rows to its width), found by looking at the todo screenshot · yes
- D-49 · GTK test windows ignore real input (can-focus/can-target off) and say they close by themselves · a screenshot showed keystrokes from the owner's desktop landing in a test window (they can corrupt a check and are disruptive) · yes
- D-50 · review (goal 5): a control's built-in width is now a preference (Apple: priority-750 constraint; GTK/web: dropped or a CSS variable) so the app's fill/grow/width always wins; iPhone fill uses the stack width minus padding (not the margins guide); Mac max-width labels wrap; a growing child beats justify spacers; capped+filled views centre on the web like GTK's clamp; todo width 320 fits a 375pt iPhone; M9 now measures field and list widths on both hosts · yes
- D-51 · review round 2 (goal 5): Apple built-in widths/heights are skipped when the app sizes the view (GTK's rule; a priority-750 preference had beaten grow, stretching the todo's Add button), preferred priority 760 (above compression resistance), web fill+max = width 100% + auto margins, new measured fill+max case in M3 · yes
- D-52 · review round 3 (PASS) should-fix: stacks record which size they decide for each child (fill in a VStack or grow in an HStack = width; the reverse = height); hosts drop a control's built-in width/height only for that axis; measured: a LazyList growing downwards keeps its width and takes the free height · yes

## Goal 6: GPU Data Lens
- D-53 · hybrid UI: native controls + a Canvas the app draws itself, over all-native (can't show GPU work) or all-GPU-drawn (loses native look, accessibility, text input) · owner approved 2026-10-15 · yes
- D-54 · Canvas pixels come from `App.draw(id, mut surface)` at the canvas's real pixel size, over putting pixels in the view tree like Image · a 1080p frame is 8 MB; diffing or copying it through the tree every frame is waste; `version` says when to redraw · yes
- D-55 · pan keeps a size-independent scale (data units per point) and zoom happens around the pointer · the same gestures give the same state on every host whatever the canvas size, so GTK and web can be compared exactly · yes
- D-56 · Apple builds compile the GPU path out (`not CompilationTarget.is_linux()`), over linking MAX's runtime · probe: with the guard, iOS objects have no AsyncRT symbols; MAX's Apple GPU runtime can't go into our runtime-free Apple builds yet · yes
- D-57 · web canvas: JPEG frames over a pull loop (browser asks for the next frame after drawing one) over a WebSocket push · back-pressure for free, reuses the normal event path for gestures, no new server machinery · yes
- D-58 · web frames paced by requestAnimationFrame while playing, over pulling as fast as the server answers · unpaced it pulled 145 fps (headless), wasting server GPU and bandwidth on frames no screen shows · yes
- D-59 · the web driver's `frames` step skips 10 warm-up frames · measured: one 371 ms gap as Play starts (the click's round trip), then steady 7–10 ms; the step reports the steady rate, the gap is logged in the journal · yes
- D-60 · every web tab builds its own 20M-point dataset (3.3 s, ~640 MB host + GPU) · simplest; sharing one dataset across sessions needs a process-wide cache in mojoui, a later step before any multi-user demo · yes
- D-61 · Apple allowlist and libSystem stubs gain `_sin`, `_cos`, `___sincos_stret` (libm, part of libSystem) and `_clock_gettime_nsec_np` (libSystem's clock) · the lens generates its trips with sin/cos and PointCloud times frames; all four are Apple system functions, so "only Apple system functions" still holds (same reasoning as D-22) · yes
- D-62 · Apple canvas gestures use recognisers (pan, magnification/pinch, click/tap) plus a Mojo `scrollWheel:` on a runtime NSImageView subclass, over raw mouse methods · the platform's own gesture system, as on GTK; CGPoint/CGRect returns read from d0–d3 (checked in arm64 disassembly) · yes
- D-63 · M5 import rule tightened to the project's strict one (only `mojoui`, no `std.`), with `mojoui.maths` re-exporting maths · the earlier goals' checkers already enforce it (it caught the lens); one rule, not two · yes
- D-64 · canvases tick only while needed (frames asked for, a redraw or a resize due) over ticking every display frame forever · idle canvases mustn't drain laptop and phone batteries; GTK re-arms on patches and window size changes, Apple display links pause and wake · yes
- D-65 · GTK test windows are fixed-size (non-resizable at the app's window_size) over taking whatever tile the desktop gives · checks measured different canvas sizes depending on what else was open on the owner's workspace; real (non-test) windows stay resizable · yes

## Goal 7: Android
- D-66 · the framework's Java file is an Activity plus listeners (75 lines), over a ~40-line listener used with NativeActivity · NativeActivity claims the window for native drawing (it must be handed back) and its native thread can't find app classes without class-loader tricks; an Activity that calls Mojo avoids both; still one framework file, never app code · yes
- D-67 · JNI functions called by their slot in the JNIEnv table (from the JDK's jni.h), cast per call, over generated bindings · the same trick as objc_msgSend on Apple; no C, no binding generator · yes
- D-68 · tested on the Android 36 x86_64 emulator, with the APK also carrying arm64-v8a for phones · no phone was connected; the emulator runs the x86_64 library natively under KVM · yes
- D-69 · listeners are silenced by a Java field (`quiet`) while Mojo changes views, over a Mojo field · the GTK lesson: callbacks arrive re-entrantly, so the flag must live outside Mojo · yes
- D-70 · M6 (goal 7, written this goal) measures the painted share of the canvas (≥ 25%) and a gradient (≥ 20 colours) instead of ≥ 500 colours · the first full run showed the threshold was miscalibrated: 2M points on the CPU give a few dozen density levels (39 colours) on a fully drawn map (screenshot checked); not a weakened earlier check · yes
- D-71 · CPU-only builds link two empty stand-ins for MAX's release functions (mojoui/hosts/no_gpu_rt.mojo), over restructuring PointCloud's fields · the GPU fields are always empty there but their destructors name those functions once the app can be freed (onDestroy); same approach as mojo_rt.mojo · yes
- D-72 · Android content fits the system bars (setFitsSystemWindows) and centres with flexible space, over gravity centring · Android 15+ draws edge to edge for targetSdk ≥ 35, and gravity-centred content taller than the screen loses its top under the bars · yes

## Goal 8: analytics showcase
- D-73 · "Flint" read as Fluent 2 (Microsoft's design system) · the owner named Microsoft's design language; Fluent 2's tokens (neutrals, brand blue, radii, type ramp, elevation) are public and fit a data app · yes
- D-74 · charts drawn by Mojo on Canvas views, text from a baked font atlas, over native chart widgets · there's no common native chart widget (Swift Charts on Apple only); one renderer looks identical everywhere and later runs on the GPU · yes
- D-75 · money summed as 64-bit integer cents with atomics, over float sums · exact: GPU = CPU to the cent (probe: 20M rows, 64 groups, 1.9 ms) · yes
- D-76 · correction: "Flint" is Microsoft's Flint chart language (the owner sent https://microsoft.github.io/flint-chart/), not a design system · charts are declared as Flint specs (ChartAssemblyInput: chartType, encodings, semantic types), rendered natively by mojoui (a Mojo backend for Flint) and exported as JSON that Flint's validateChart accepts; Fluent 2 (D-73) stays as the controls' look; GOAL.md M3 updated (the owner's clarification, not a weakened check) · yes
- D-77 · Flint specs are checked by compiling them through Flint's own Vega-Lite backend (any Flint warning fails), then Vega-Lite → Vega → SVG that must show the data's labels, over `validateChart` · flint-chart 0.5.1 on npm doesn't export validateChart yet (its docs are ahead); the reviewer showed compile-only proved little, hence warnings and labels; GOAL.md M3 wording updated to match · yes
- D-78 · the month chart and year-on-year use every month of every year (the year filter ignored), the chart slicing to the selected year · picking a year then a month compared against a month the query had filtered out ("First year of data"); fixed at the query, not the label · yes
- D-79 · goal 9 (the Workbook): columns not cells; Numbers-style objects on a Board, no `=B3*C3` · the owner chose it ("being like Excel and not being Excel is a good thing"); a column DAG is dozens of nodes, a cell DAG millions · yes
- D-80 · shared read-only data per process (`App.init_shared()`), sessions keep a sparse edit overlay; the GPU aggregates the base, edit deltas are applied on the CPU; formula columns are virtual (bytecode in the kernel and per visible row) · the owner asked for shared buffers across web sessions before wasm; per-session copies of 20M rows (640 MB host + GPU) made the web host one-tab-only; deltas are exact and O(edits); virtual formulas cost no memory per session · yes
- D-81 · the web host serialises calls into the Mojo module with one process-wide engine lock when shared data is in use · two sessions share one DeviceContext and one GpuFrame, which aren't known to be thread-safe (each query allocates its own scratch, so that part is fine); the GPU is serial anyway · yes (revisit when a thread-safety guarantee exists)
- D-82 · Parquet through pyarrow from Python (Linux + web) · Mojo has no Parquet writer; MX frame's Mojo API (spec items 13–22) replaces it · yes
- D-83 · the GPU group-by interprets a small bytecode per row (key, value, filters chosen at run time) instead of compile-time functions · pivots and formula columns are chosen by the person at run time; the interpreter costs nothing measurable once the stack lives in registers (0.8–1.9 ms on 20M rows) · yes
- D-84 · `mojoui/contoso.mojo` holds the sample generator as typed columns (9 columns: day, category, region, channel, product, quantity, cents, cost, year); the analytics app keeps its own copy until M8 converts it · one source of sample data for tests, the workbook and (after M8) the dashboard · yes
- D-85 · the shared-data registry is the process environment (`MOJOUI_SHARED_<slot>` = address) · Mojo 1.1 has no global variables; every host and platform has an environment; replaceable in one file (mojoui/shared.mojo) · yes
- D-86 · a Board child moves by its header (top 44 pt) and resizes by its bottom-right corner; the rest of the object keeps its own gestures (grid pans, text selects) · one drag gesture per child without stealing the grid's or the text area's · yes
- D-87 · a moved or resized object is a layout change (full rebuild), not a patch · positions are layout; rebuilds are cheap and both hosts already do them · yes (a patch later if it ever shows)
- D-88 · on GTK, a Board narrower than 720 pt is decided at build time from the window's size (no rebuild on a live resize yet); the web uses a CSS media query · the check sets the size up front; live resizing is a later step · yes
- D-89 · a workbook names its data source ("contoso:20000000") instead of copying 20M rows into Parquet; only tables without a reproducible source get a Parquet file · the sample workbook ships in git at 6 KB; exports of a real table still round-trip through Parquet (tested) · yes
- D-90 · wasm through LLVM IR retargeted to wasm64 plus a C runtime shim, over a compiler fork · no fork needed: the compiler isn't the wall (LLVM has the backend); wasm64 keeps Mojo's 64-bit Int/pointers; the shim is 8 symbols · yes (wasm32 needs the compiler's help)
- D-91 · the table's sort is a radix sort over gathered column values (16 bits a pass, as many passes as the key range needs), over the standard comparison sort · the owner's first morning test: "sort becomes slow or crashes"; a comparison sort over 20M rows of 8 categories goes quadratic; now 0.45 s (category), 0.9 s (cents), 2.1 s (a formula column) on 20M rows, exact and stable · yes
- D-92 · a Board's drag gesture is attached to the Board, which finds the object under the pointer, over one gesture per object · a GTK gesture measures from the widget it's attached to, so a gesture on the object it moves measures against itself and jitters ("moving the cards is jittery"); the web adapter already measured from the viewport · yes
- D-93 · an object's move (and the Board's own size) is a patch (SET_PLACE), not a rebuild; a resize still rebuilds · supersedes D-87: the rebuild after every drop re-created every canvas, which read as a flash; the web applies the patch through a hidden "place" marker inside the object (no script in the response: htmx doesn't run a bare script with swap none) · yes
- D-94 · cards are added from one **Insert…** dropdown in the top bar and removed by a Remove button in each card's header, over four + buttons · the owner asked how to add a card; four buttons wrapped the bar to two lines on the web and pushed the note's corner off a 1440×900 page; each pivot now has its own dropdown messages (two pivots used to change together) · yes
- D-95 · GTK checks run on a headless X display (Xvfb from the pixi environment, `scripts/headless.py`: X11 backend, cairo renderer, GL off), over showing test windows on the desktop · the owner: "it keeps popping up a window"; shown windows also lost frame-clock ticks when another window covered them (goal 6's fps check failed with "no FRAMES line" during a parallel run); Xvfb gives screenshots and a 60 fps frame clock with nothing on screen; `MOJOUI_HEADLESS=0` shows the windows again · yes
- D-96 · the "drawn, not blank" checks measure the painted share (pixels away from the background: goal 6's lens canvas on GTK and the web ≥ 15%, goal 8's analytics page ≥ 8%), replacing colour counts · the owner's decision (2026-10-16, "go with your recommendations"): colour counts depended on the renderer's text antialiasing (NVIDIA GL on the desktop vs cairo headless) and on JPEG vs PNG frames; painted share is what the eye checks (D-70 did the same for Android); measured: lens canvas 46% (GTK), analytics page 16% · yes

## Goal 10: the Workbook, usable
- D-97 · the chrome floats in islands over a scrolling page (`Overlay` + `.pin(h, v)`), and Insert is four toolbar buttons (Table, Pivot, Chart, Note) instead of the Insert… menu · the owner asked for a theme that "looks good", picked by us; their taste file says canvas apps get "chrome as small floating islands over the canvas"; buttons are found without opening a menu; goal 9's archived steps follow the new controls (same actions: tap:Note instead of select:0=Note; the pivot's dropdowns are 0 and 1 again) · yes
- D-98 · the default page is 1232 pt wide (fits a 1280-pt laptop window with its margins), not 1360 · the owner's taste file: "check at 1440×900 and 1280×800: everything fits"; at 1360 the table's Sort button was off-screen at 1280; goal 9's archived tap positions move with the columns (the same cents cell, the same note size check) · yes
- D-99 · the grid's scroll bar is drawn by the app on its canvas and driven by a new `PRESS` gesture (pointer down, sent before any TAP or PAN), over a native scroll bar · 20M rows can't be a native scrolled list; one renderer looks the same on every host; PRESS is how a canvas knows a drag started on the thumb (apps that don't use it ignore it: all existing apps check the gesture kind) · yes
- D-100 · Insert puts the card in the first free spot on the page and the Board scrolls it into view (`Board(…, reveal=k, reveal_count=n)`), over always appending at the bottom · the owner couldn't find an inserted chart; a reveal is done once per count, on both hosts, and the web marks the card with a 1.2 s fade ring (laptop motion: a short fade only) · yes
- D-101 · the theme's icons are Lucide everywhere: the web inlines the SVGs, GTK writes the same SVGs (stroke colour filled in) to a cache folder and loads them through its icon theme, over Adwaita's symbolic icons · Adwaita has no table, pivot, chart or sticky-note icons, and mixing two icon sets in one toolbar looks wrong; the 14 older icon names keep their Adwaita mapping · yes
- D-102 · formatting is planned column-first (column formats, then rules, then sparse cell overrides), in `docs/plans/formatting.md`, not built in goal 10 · the owner: "fix the basics, then formatting"; per-cell formats on 20M rows would cost what the overlay design avoids · yes
- D-103 · Insert searches for free space from the top of the page (left to right, 16-pt steps) and then scrolls there, over "the top-left of the visible part of the page" (GOAL.md's assumption) · the app doesn't know the host's scroll position, and asking for it would add a host→app channel for one feature; filling gaps near the top keeps pages compact, and the reveal shows the card wherever it lands (the reviewer flagged the departure) · yes
- D-104 · canvas drags end with a `RELEASE` gesture (sent only after a drag, not after a tap) · the reviewer found the grid's thumb drag could outlive its drag (a dropped PRESS on GTK while busy left `bar_drag` set, so a later grid pan jumped); the app now lets go on RELEASE; the lens ignores PRESS and RELEASE so they never redraw its 20M points · yes
- D-105 · a design language `OMARCHY` that reads the person's current Omarchy theme file at start (colours, light/dark) and draws with JetBrains Mono, flat controls and hairline borders, on both hosts and inside the canvases (`mojoui.theme.Palette`, threaded through the Flint charts); the Workbook uses it, the analytics showcase stays Fluent · the owner: "default it to Omarchy… JetBrains Mono and flat themes… use that everywhere"; reading the theme (not copying Tokyo Night) means it follows a theme switch on the next start; Tokyo Night is the fallback · yes

With them: the licence these versions ship under (Modular MAX Community License, from the conda packages; the PyPI wheels name a licence and carry no text), the third-party notices, and our NOTICE beside the app and in the bundle (the §1.1.4 attribution, adapted for unmodified code; where the licence text is; the app's own code MIT, Modular's libraries under Modular's terms). The MAX License of 2026-10-21 (§1.2) and the earlier one both let an application redistribute MAX in object code form. libAsyncRTMojoBindings' source (MLRT) is MAX-licensed, so a modified fork may not be published and no AI may reimplement it from that source: the bundle is the allowed route, and nothing of Modular's is copied into omxcode's source. Open: whether these four are "Redistributable Components" per Modular's documentation, and which licence text applies to pre-2026-10-21 versions (a question drafted in docs/forum/modular-license-runtime.md, not posted: the owner, 2026-10-25, is content with the licence included; if Modular ever objects, the link goes back to the CPU build). ## Goal 33: OMX Console, rebuilt in omxcode (2026-10-25)
- D-310 · HTTP is our own small HTTP/1.1 client over libc sockets behind `mojoui.http`, not Ehsan Kermani's flare (MIT, v0.13, HTTP/1–3, TLS) or lightbug_http · flare needs Mojo ≥ 1.2 (we pin 1.1), builds C shims and OpenSSL, and targets Linux and macOS only; the console only talks plain HTTP to 127.0.0.1; flare can sit behind the same API once we move to Mojo 1.2 and need TLS · reversible? yes
- D-311 · goal 33 goes ahead of goal 32's M3–M7 (owner: "this comes first… if we can't build apps… why bother advertising"); goal 32 parked at M0–M2 PASS, its contract and checker archived to docs/goals/32-GOAL.md and 32-check.py; M8 reruns goal 32's passing milestones · reversible? yes

- D-315 · chose create_app with its test-double hooks (api_deps), run under mxstudio's own Python, over `mxc daemon` · the CLI can't take a fake GPU, catalog or model card, and without them search needs `max` and the notebook needs Hugging Face; it's the same app code · reversible? yes
- D-316 · chose to seed models straight into the store (sparse weights, a max-serve log) over pulling them through the fake hub · fake_hub's speed is one setting for every pull, and the one pull left running has to stay mid-way while the screens are recorded · reversible? yes

- D-318 · chose a per-channel tolerance of 8/255 (a pixel differs if any channel moves more than 8) over exact equality · anti-aliasing can move a channel a few steps between renderers; M7 uses the same function; measured floor 0.000 % · reversible? yes
- D-319 · chose fixtures as the raw reply bodies (JSON re-indented, values unchanged; logs as .txt) plus _manifest.json (request → file, status, type, kept headers) over one envelope file per reply · M2's json.mojo reads the real replies as they are · reversible? yes
- D-320 · mojoui.http is a generic component with a transport seam (owner, 2026-10-25: "this is a generic framework, it should support both… the http component should be generic"): requests, replies as Events and the `Http` outbox know nothing of the transport; a `Transport` trait with one blocking `send(req) -> Response`, run on a worker; today's backend is `SocketTransport` (plain HTTP/1.1, http:// only; an https:// URL is a clear error Event); flare goes behind the same trait as one file and a `-D MOJOUI_FLARE` switch in mojoui/effects.mojo, no app changes (docs/http.md); refines D-310 · reversible? yes
- D-321 · chose an app's opt-in (`comptime HTTP = True`) over always linking the transport · an app without it has no socket or pthread calls in its binary, so the Apple stubs and goal 5's ALLOWED list stay as they are for every existing app; its requests come back as an error Event saying what to set · reversible? yes
- D-322 · chose requests as data the app hands over (`requests()` returns its `Http` outbox's take(), read after every update: Elm's Cmd) over a global "current runtime" or a new update() signature · no existing app changes, and the web's many sessions in one process share nothing · reversible? yes
- D-323 · chose our own detached pthread per request with a two-owner count (the handle and the worker, as mxframe's jobs) over mxframe's start_job, and hosts that poll the runtime (Runtime.wake_in, poll) over a wake-up pipe · mxframe's jobs run inline on Apple targets unless -D MXFRAME_JOB_THREADS, which would hold the Mac's UI for a whole request; polling needs no new fd plumbing per host: GTK arms one GLib timeout (replies every 4 ms below the redraw priority, so frames never wait; timers at the default priority), the web a clock thread plus a /wait long-poll the page keeps open only while the app is busy, the Mac performSelector:afterDelay: · reversible? yes
- D-324 · chose declared timers (`App.every()` lists `Every(ms, msg)`, read after every update: Elm's subscriptions) over start/stop calls · a timer can't outlive the state that wants it; ticks keep to the start's grid (start + k × ms), so they don't drift · reversible? yes
- D-325 · chose an exact fast path plus libc strtod_l in the "C" locale for json.mojo's decimals (mxframe's csv approach) over Mojo's atof · atof was one ulp off on 5 of 3,000 doubles (tests/fixtures/json/floats.json); strtod_l rounds correctly and ignores a host's setlocale · reversible? yes

## Goal 35: AG-UI, agents that build the screen
- D-700 · a stream is a Request flag (`.stream(END)`): pieces come back as the request's own msg, the end as a second message, over one message with a "done" marker or a new App method · an SSE event may be named anything, so no in-band marker is safe; no change to the App trait · yes
- D-701 · the worker hands pieces to the UI thread through a singly linked list it alone writes (atomic next pointers, the runtime frees what it read), over a mutex-guarded queue · Mojo 1.1's Atomic load/store is all it needs, no pthread mutex declarations to clash · yes
- D-702 · `Http.cancel(id)` ends the request at the runtime's next poll (its end Event with "cancelled"), the worker stopping on its own within 50 ms, over waiting for the worker · nothing of a cancelled stream reaches update() after its end, deterministically · yes
- D-703 · a stream's timeout is its longest silence, over the whole request's · an agent run may stream for minutes; silence is what a dead server looks like · yes
- D-704 · httpcheck gains a Stream button (frames counted while 20 events trickle in) for M0's "frames keep drawing" on GTK and the web, over a new example · httpcheck already measures frames for requests; goal 33's labels are unchanged · yes
- D-705 · JSON Patch on our own mutable JSON tree (`JDoc`, numbers kept as their literal text, a patch applied to a copy that replaces the document only when every op succeeded), over patching mojoui.json's parse tree · the parse tree is read-only by design; atomicity comes free with the copy; 37 cases agree with the jsonpatch library · yes
- D-706 · the AG-UI event reference is the SDK's own source (ag_ui/_generated/models.py, encoder: `data: <json>\n\n`), the docs' event page as a cross-check, over the docs alone · the docs page names events in PascalCase only; the wire names come from the schema-generated models · yes
- D-707 · the recorded streams are regenerated by the check with the SDK and must match the committed ones byte for byte, and the check reads them with its own Python reducer (jsonpatch for deltas), over trusting the fixtures · proves both "from the official SDK" and "kept the same conversation and state" independently of the Mojo code · yes
- D-708 · a frontend tool call stays open until the app answers it (Agent.pending/answer), and the answer rides the next run as a tool message, over auto-answering · AG-UI's own pattern; it is what lets a button in agent-built UI be the tool's result (M2) · yes
- D-709 · one catalog in mojoui/genui.mojo drives the JSON Schema agents get, the validator and the renderer (13 catalogued components; no WebComponent, WebFrame, Canvas, Image), over a hand-written schema beside the code · they can't drift; jsonschema agrees with the validator on all 24 cases; limits a schema can't express (8 levels, 200 components, 32 KB) are in its description and enforced by the app · yes
- D-711 · inputs in agent-built UI bind to a JSON pointer in the shared state (edits reach the agent with its next run); the first action (a button, or a choice with an action) is render_ui's tool result, later ones a user message; a UI nobody used is answered {"shown": true} before the next user message · AG-UI keeps every tool call answered; the agent hears exactly what the user did · yes
- D-712 · refused UI is answered at once with {"error": "refused: <why>"} and the agent runs again, and the chat shows a warning card instead of the UI · the agent can correct itself; nothing of the refused UI is drawn · yes
- D-713 · the web host lets a text field given a size (width, grow, fill) take it: `--control-max:100%` beside `--control-width`, over the 70vw cap · the chat's composer stopped at 70 % of the window; fields without a size keep the cap · yes
- D-710 · the live agent speaks the OpenAI-compatible chat API (base URL, model and the key's variable NAME from env; the OMX Console at 127.0.0.1:11435/v1 with qwen2.5-0.5b by default), with Claude through the Anthropic SDK as an option (AGUI_LLM_PROVIDER=anthropic, claude-opus-5-5) · owner, 2026-10-27: one code path for local models and Claude, no cloud by default · yes
- D-714 · the backend repairs a model's render_ui JSON (fences, quotes, trailing commas, literals, case and synonyms of component and prop names, a bare node wrapped, UI written as words lifted out) and shows the model a short schema plus an explicit component list, while the app validates the full schema · small local models break tool calls; the repair widens what works without widening what's accepted · yes

- D-716 · merge of origin/main (7b61640, release-33 with goal 34b's reviewer follow-ups) into goal-35-agui: both branches had numbered from D-677/D-680, so goal 35's D-680–D-695 became D-700–D-715 (decisions, journal, EXPERIMENTS.md, docs/agui.md, mojoui/http.mojo, jsonpatch.mojo, genui.mojo, scripts/agui_agent.py); D-677–D-682 keep 34b's meaning. docs/goals/34b-GOAL.md and 34b-check.py are 34b's final versions (archived with the ROOT line fix) · reversible? yes
- D-717 · a JDoc stays within 100,000 values, 32 levels and 4 MB of JSON (read, patch, set, snapshot): checked per op (a quick count, then a full measure at the end) and refused atomically; numbers that aren't finite are refused, whole numbers past 2^53 written as decimals; `set` on an array replaces an existing item and refuses "-" or an index past the end (reviewer: copy bombs, deep adds, `"state":,`, binds inserting per keystroke) · bounds an agent can't push past; the caps are generous for UI state · yes

- D-770 (goal 38, fair play) · the shared harness drives build D through `dashboard --scripted-external --rows 10M|100M --out DIR`: stdin lines `step N` (0 … 32) apply steps.json's state N, `quit` or stdin's end writes answers.json and timings.json once the last step asked for is drawn; stdout speaks the harness's driver contract (`ready <json>`, `shown N <ms> <json>` with gpu_ms and compute_ms, `error <text>`, `bye`; docs/goals/38/README.md; examples/dashboard/README.md) · stdin over a local HTTP POST: no server code, no port to pick, and the line is read on the GTK main loop the moment it arrives (new generic `App.input_lines` → `g_unix_fd_add` on fd 0), so the drive adds no polling delay of its own; the app stamps nothing beyond its existing in-app timings · yes
- D-771 (goal 38, coordinator: "the real framework latency, not a frame of polling") · a background job wakes the UI the moment it ends: `mojoui.jobs.start_ui_job` (mxframe's `start_job_then` with a completion callback) writes a byte to a self-pipe; GTK watches it with `g_unix_fd_add` (priority DEFAULT, ahead of the redraw), the web host with a thread blocking on it that runs every tab's update() and pushes as effects do (pending + /wait); the app names the message (`App.jobs_message`, default 0: unchanged). On Apple the engine runs jobs serially on the main thread, so the callback asks NSApp's delegate (the AppKit target) for `jobsEnded:` on the next run-loop turn (common modes): no new C symbols for the Apple stubs. UIKit not wired (no `jobsEnded:` there: the callback checks `respondsToSelector:` and the frame poll stays). The per-frame poll stays the fallback; `MOJOUI_JOB_WAKE=0` turns the GTK wake off to compare · a pipe over g_idle_add from the worker: one mechanism every host can watch (fd), no GLib call from a foreign thread, and the worker only writes a byte · yes

Its M5 now checks results.json against the commit it was measured from (793e54a, goal-38-data's tip, an ancestor after the merge: its tree gives the recorded sources_sha256) and lets today's tree differ from it only inside mojoui/, naming the files that do · data_bench.py's hash covers all of mojoui/, so the release's own framework changes (web.py, look.mojo, shared.mojo) staled the published results the moment they met; re-measuring takes hours on a quiet desktop (owner), and pinning to the measured commit is what goal 33's check does for the console (D-760) · yes
- D-783 · omxcore 834ef9a vendored (stables 2585dc9, 8017543, 5053a44, 5e4cdb1, 834ef9a since 3d2455d) under the engine's own name: vendor/omxcore.mojoc and vendor/OMXCORE_COMMIT, `from omxcore import` in every module and test, examples' needs files say omxcore (scripts/build.sh links the codecs on that word), engine_lineage.py (build/omxcore-src, the lineage through 834ef9a) and gpu_audit.py read the new names, over keeping mxframe.mojoc · D-308 left the rename as debt, and build_mojo_pkg.sh builds mxframe.mojoc "for one stable release" only: the next stable drops it. Built from a clean `git archive 834ef9a` with this repo's Mojo 1.1 (the package's warnings name the scratch folder, harmless). mojoui/mxframe.mojo (the app's own analytics wrapper) keeps its name: it is app code, not the package. The omxcore checkout is found from $OMXCORE_REPO (or $MXFRAME_REPO), else ../../mxframe, else ~/Projects/mxframe (D-755's lesson). Mac: no new libSystem symbols (STABLE.md); the Linux cross-build links against apple/sdk's stubs unchanged · yes
- D-784 · formula columns are undone through omxcore's own undo log (2585dc9: adding, replacing and removing a formula are Frame steps), over the sheet's second log for them · with both, a formula step counted twice in undo_depth, and an undo that re-applied the older formulas recorded new package steps, so undoing two formulas brought the first one back (tests/test_streamed.mojo: two formulas, a replacement and a format change between them undo one by one, newest first, depth 4 to 0, nothing returns; it failed with depth 7 before). Sheet.formula() is the package's; undo_drops() and last_undone read the package's next step (the formula that appears, changes or goes against the step's saved list) · yes
- D-785 · gpu_audit judges a launch-sized kernel (external_memory) by a bound its file names and enforces (a comparison against the bound or a constant built from it: reviewer round 1) ("bound: SMEM_LIMIT", "bound: GROUP_SMEM_LIMIT") worked out for an Apple GPU (a `(32 if <Apple condition> else 48) * 1024` choice reads its Apple side; a negated condition is not read), besides literal "<= N * 1024" budgets, over treating them as unsizable · omxcore#19 (F-195) made those limits per-target constants and named them at the kernels; 834ef9a's launches enforce them (run_pipeline_gpu refuses past SMEM_LIMIT with -5, group_shared_bytes caps at SHARED_MAX_GROUPS = GROUP_SMEM_LIMIT // 4). Not weaker: a bound that isn't named, or whose condition isn't Apple's, still leaves the kernel unsized and the audit failing; 3d2455d (the engine before the fix) still fails it (both kernels unsized), 834ef9a passes (32,768 B each) · yes

The main checkout (a real folder, which git already showed as a deleted symlink) is unaffected · yes

- D-789 · mojoui.devgroup's shared accumulators drop from 4096 to 3584 int64s (28 KiB), so with its 4 KiB spec the kernel fits 32 KiB, every GPU's floor (Apple's), over exempting it as NVIDIA-only · goal 32 M6's audit, run on the combined release, found 36,864 B (goal 38 wrote devgroup before omxcore 834ef9a's device bounds). Groups × (sums + 1) up to 3584 still add up in shared memory, more go to global atomics as before; the dashboard's product × day (29,240 groups) was already on the global path. test_devgroup passes; a provisional 10M dashboard run: every pass equal to DuckDB's.
- D-790 · the README's Try it says how to recover when `pixi install` stops at `failed to link 'share/X11/xkb'` (`rm -rf .pixi && pixi install`), over moving Xvfb out of the default env on launch day · conda-forge's xorg-xvfb-server ships share/X11/xkb as a folder and its own dependency xkeyboard-config ships it as a symlink; which links first decides it (3 fresh clones of the release: 1 failed, 2 passed; a retry on the failed env fails again, a new env passes). - D-791 · (owner, 2026-10-30: "show the world… it follows the best practice") accessible names: a `View.label()` field plus one rule in mojoui.a11y (label, tooltip, the control's own words, a placeholder) that every host applies, over a per-host rule or reusing `.tag()` (tags are identifiers for tests and CSS); a new label is a new layout (labels rarely change), a drawn view's changing words stay its tooltip (SET_TIP, a patch) · hosts were naming things differently (GTK: tooltips only; Android: the test tag) · reversible: yes

The Metal (GPU) build gets the switch too, unrun with it. iPhone unchanged (no wake on UIKit, never run on hardware). `MOJOUI_SERIAL_JOBS=1` builds the old way to compare · reversible: yes

- D-804 · VideoSource reads ffmpeg through libc popen/fread (one long-lived `ffmpeg -f rawvideo -pix_fmt rgba -` per read position), over Mojo's std.subprocess.run (text only, waits for the exit), posix_spawn with pipes (more code, same effect) and a libav binding (a new dependency; D-805 keeps the door open) · it measured 500 frames/s at 720p (goal: 240), works the same on Linux and Mac, and the caller sees only open/next/seek/read; each start runs one /bin/sh, so paths are single-quoted · yes

- D-806 · seek restarts ffmpeg with `-ss (n − 0.5)/fps` before `-i` (decode from the keyframe before it, drop up to the time), over `-ss` exactly n/fps · half a frame early means frame n is the first one at or after the time whatever the container rounds to (29.97 fps files included); the test compares seeks to ffmpeg's `select=eq(n,…)` decode byte for byte · yes
- D-807 · frame count = the container's nb_frames, else duration × fps; the test checks it against ffprobe's -count_frames (decoded) · yes

- D-810 · mojoui.imageops gathers only the rectangles being edited into a compact buffer, sends those to the GPU and scatters them back, over uploading and downloading the whole 1280×720 frame · blur is clamped to its rectangle and text/boxes only touch theirs, so the pixels outside never need to move; four 120×120 boxes are 230 KB instead of 3.7 MB each way, which is what puts "blur 720p" at 0.5 ms (it still includes the upload and download of everything that changes) · yes
- D-811 · box blur ×3 (a horizontal then a vertical pass, three times) with the edge pixel repeated at the rectangle's edge, over a true gaussian or a pixelate option · three box passes are within a few percent of a gaussian, need only integer sums, and are one tiny kernel; pixelate wasn't asked for beyond "or" · yes
- D-812 · GPU and CPU run the same integer pixel functions (`_blur_px`, `_scale_px`, `_compose_px`): the kernels are thin wrappers and the CPU path is the same function in a loop over the same gathered buffers, over writing each path separately and checking with a tolerance · the check is max channel difference ≤ 1 and the integer arithmetic gives 0; bilinear uses 1/256 fixed-point weights for that reason · yes
- D-813 · captions, rectangles and outlines are rasterised on the CPU into a coverage map (mojoui.text's Font drawing white on black, mojoui.draw.rect for shapes) and the GPU only composites it by colour, over uploading the font atlas and rasterising glyphs in a kernel · reuses the framework's font as asked (no second font system), a caption is a few KB so the CPU raster costs microseconds, and the composite is identical on both paths · yes (move glyph raster to the GPU if captions become large or hundreds per frame)
- D-814 · `Edits` is an ordered list applied by `ImageOps.apply`, which groups consecutive edits that don't overlap into one GPU trip (one upload, all kernels, one download) and gives an overlapping edit its own trip, over one trip per call · a video frame gets several boxes and a caption at once; order only matters where rectangles overlap, so independent edits batch and results equal doing them one by one · yes

- D-821 · decode on the draw call, one frame at a time (500 frames/s at 720p, a few ms), reading on for a jump of up to 12 frames and seeking beyond, over a decode-ahead thread · decoding is far under the 33 ms budget so a thread is a second place for the picture to be wrong; a drag of the scrubber costs one ffmpeg restart per drawn frame (~60-100 ms), the slowest path and the one to revisit with keyframe thumbnails if it feels slow · yes
- D-822 · playback follows the display's clock: each display frame adds its elapsed time (at most 0.25 s) to the media clock and the picture is the frame at that time, so a slow frame is dropped, never stretched; t() is always the shown frame's time (frame / fps), the cursor and the trace read it · "drop frames rather than drift"; one time for the picture, the chart and PLAYER/CURSOR lines is what makes them agree · yes

- D-1308 · (M1) the list holds every note's title in memory (a Dict by id) and the search returns ids only (FTS5 MATCH on `"word"*` per typed word, mojoui.sqlite.fts_prefix_query), newest first, over asking SQLite for each row LazyList shows · App.row() is read-only (no statement can step there), and 10,000 titles are ~0.5 MB; quoting every word means FTS5's syntax is searched for, never run · yes
- D-1309 · (M2) the GTK `gaps:` step became a freeze detector of its own: gaps:start shows a test window that isn't shown, listens to its paints (as MOJOUI_TRACE does) and asks for a redraw on every frame until gaps:N ends; it prints `GAPS: start`. Over making Jot animate something while it seeds · without MOJOUI_TRACE or MOJOUI_SHOT the step counted nothing (0 frames: an unshown window never paints), and a window with nothing moving doesn't paint either, so idle time read as gaps; with a redraw asked each frame only a busy UI thread makes one. Goal 30's runs (traced and shown already) now also get a redraw per frame; their checker wasn't re-run (stated in the report) · yes
- D-1310 · (M2) the seed starts on the first 50 ms tick at least 600 ms after the app was made (the window opens with what the file holds, then fills), over starting it in __init__ · 10,000 notes take ~150 ms in one job; started in __init__ it finished before the window could be measured. 600 ms is generous on this machine, not a guarantee: `GAPS: start` printing before `JOT SEED: start` is what shows the measure covered it (checked by hand; the checker only checks the end) · yes
- D-1311 · (M3) mojoui.sqlite looks dlopen itself up (dlsym, RTLD_DEFAULT) and calls it by address, over an external_call["dlopen"] or the standard library's OwnedDLHandle · a program may declare a C function once: GTK's host (present.mojo, gtk_webview.mojo) declares dlopen (pointer, int) → int, the std's OwnedDLHandle (used by the web's Python module) another way, so either declaration broke one host's build (both seen). The Mac's libSystem stub gains _dlclose, _dlerror, _setenv, _time (real libSystem exports: mojoui.shared's put_shared and Jot's clock needed them) · yes
- D-1312 · (M3) FileSaver hands the app a path and the app writes the file in update(), over the host taking bytes from the app · one call shape on every host: GTK's save dialog answers a path; the web host answers a path in the page's downloads folder (its suggested name) and, when the app wrote it, the page downloads it (an i.mojoui-download appended by an out-of-band swap, a MutationObserver clicks a download link); the Mac (no NSOpenPanel for FilePicker yet either) has neither (❌ in the matrix) · yes
- D-1313 · (M3) external files arrive through the DropTarget the app already has, as `DropTarget(view, on_drop, on_file=…)` once per file with the path, over a new FileDrop view · a list that takes a dragged row can take a file too; GTK adds a second GtkDropTarget for GdkFileList, the web uploads each dropped File (/dropfile) and sends its path. The `dropfile:Tag=/path[,…]` step builds a real GdkFileList and emits the GtkDropTarget's own "drop" signal (as GTK does on a drop), the closest a headless run gets to a file manager; drive.py's `dropfile:` dispatches a browser drop event carrying a File, and `save:` takes the real download · yes
- D-1314 · (M4) the quit hook is `App.closing()`, told once (Runtime.closing) whichever way the app ends: GTK the window's close-request and, if that never came, the application's shutdown (Ctrl+Q, the end of a test run); the web when a session ends (evicted, or every live one at the server's exit, atexit); the Mac applicationWillTerminate: and windowWillClose: (built, not run). It runs synchronously on the UI thread; over a "may I close?" answer · a pending save is milliseconds and must finish before the process goes; asking (Save? Discard?) is a later, separate piece. The web doesn't hear a tab closing (a session lives until evicted): stated in the matrix · yes

## Goal 45: Keeper, still working when the window is closed (2026-10-30)
- D-1600 · the checker fixes names the goal leaves open (`WATCH READY`, `WATCH THREAD:`, an `at=<monotonic ns>` on every `WATCH EVENT`/`KEEPER INDEXED` line, `files_fts(path, …)`, a tiny `examples/traycheck` for M1, a generic `waitopen:N` step and a `WINDOW: open` host line) over leaving them to the build · "lag on one clock" is only checkable if the code prints when its event arrived; the checker computes every lag itself from its own close times · yes
- D-1601 · mojoui.watch hands events from its thread to the consumer through a pipe (a tab-separated line each, 1 MB pipe) over a shared queue with a lock · nothing but the pipe crosses threads, the write blocks rather than drops when the consumer is behind, and a consumer can sleep on its fd · yes
- D-1602 · a file is reported once, when its writer closes it (IN_CREATE marks it new, IN_CLOSE_WRITE reports CREATED or MODIFIED), over reporting IN_CREATE and IN_MODIFY as they come · "none twice for one write"; a file still open after 2 s is reported anyway (then MODIFIED at its close) · yes
- D-1603 · a folder that appears gets its watch at once and is then listed; files already in it wait for their close or 20 ms (whichever first) over reporting them as found · a file opened before the watch and closed after would otherwise be reported twice; 20 ms bounds the lag for files that were already closed · yes
- D-1604 · mojoui.watch wakes the host itself (one byte down mojoui.jobs' wake pipe, slot 90) over importing mojoui.jobs · mojoui.jobs imports the engine (omxcore), which `mojo run` can't load; the watcher must run under `mojo run` for its test · yes
- D-1605 · tests/test_watch.mojo's own `ms=` estimate comes from file system timestamps (the file's ctime, its folder's for a delete) moved onto the monotonic clock · the test can't see the checker's close times; the checker judges its own lags and only asks the printed ones to agree · yes
- D-1606 · (checker addition) the private bus runs from its own config with no service directories, and without WAYLAND_DISPLAY/DISPLAY; a run that starts any service on it FAILs · with the system's session config GTK's portal request started xdg-desktop-portal on the private bus, and it reached the owner's Wayland display: exactly what the goal forbids · yes
- D-1607 · background mode is `App.background() -> Bool` plus `App.window_shown(shown)`; the GTK host hides the window on close-request (returns TRUE), holds the GApplication, and publishes the tray item at start; `closing()` then runs only when the process really ends · the smallest change to App that keeps every existing app as it was (the default is False) · yes
- D-1608 · mojoui.dbus talks GDBus by address (dlopen libgio RTLD_GLOBAL when not linked) with GVariant text (g_variant_parse) over variadic g_variant_new · one C declaration rule, works in a GTK app and under `mojo run`; quoting is the only escaping needed · yes
- D-1609 · mojoui.watch starts its thread through pthread_create looked up by address · the engine (omxcore) declares pthread_create with pointer types and a program may declare a C function once; Keeper links both · yes
- D-1610 · (checker fix, not a weakening) M2's summary printed the search's count as the scan's (a reused variable); the summary now names the scan's own count; no test changed · yes
- D-1611 · (review round 1) a folder renamed inside the watched tree emits DELETED (is_dir) for its old name, then CREATED for every file under its new name (files still waiting for their close are re-keyed and keep waiting) · a consumer knew those files by their old paths; before, a rename of a folder emitted nothing and Keeper's index kept dead paths · yes
- D-1612 · on IN_Q_OVERFLOW the watcher reports what was waiting (CREATED), flushes a waiting move, re-adds every folder's watch (add_tree on the root) and then emits OVERFLOW · the dropped events may include new folders, which would otherwise stay unwatched; the consumer rescans after OVERFLOW · yes
- D-1613 · a folder inotify_add_watch refuses (anything but ENOENT/ENOTDIR/ELOOP: EACCES, ENOSPC, …) is counted: `partial()`, `missed()`, and `why()` naming the cause (ENOSPC: raise fs.inotify.max_user_watches) · it used to be a silent `continue`; the watcher keeps watching the rest rather than failing whole · yes
- D-1614 · watcher records are length-prefixed (`<kind><d|f> <at> <path bytes> <old bytes>\n` then the raw bytes) over tab-separated lines; names that aren't UTF-8 arrive with `exact=False`, a lossy `path` and the real `path_bytes` · a name with a newline or tab could drop or forge events; Mojo's String must hold UTF-8 for display, the bytes are kept for whoever needs the real name · yes
- D-1615 · dropping a FolderWatcher stops its thread and waits for it (≤ 2 s, emptying the pipe so a blocked write returns) before closing the read end; SIGPIPE is blocked on the watcher thread and EPIPE stops it; F_SETPIPE_SZ falls back to 256 KB and the size is kept (`pipe_size`) · closing the read end under a writing thread killed the process with SIGPIPE · yes
- D-1616 · Mac: the FSEvents stream is stopped, invalidated, its queue drained with `dispatch_sync_f` (a running callback finishes), then stream and queue released, before the words it writes to are freed · the callback could run on freed memory; built, not run · yes
- D-1617 · a test-only `hold(on)` stops the watcher thread reading, so tests/test_watch_edges.mojo can overflow the kernel's queue (20,000 events past max_queued_events 16,384) without root · the only way to cause a real IN_Q_OVERFLOW unprivileged · yes
- D-1618 · (review round 1; checker addition) background mode hides the window on close only while the tray item is registered (a StatusNotifierWatcher is on the bus, watched with g_bus_watch_name_on_connection, and its RegisterStatusNotifierItem replied without an error); otherwise closing ends the app and says so. The item registers again whenever a watcher appears (a panel started or restarted). M1 gains: a run with no watcher must exit on close; the checker plays the panel (a pretend org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher in system Python's GObject bindings, on the private bus only) and must see the item register; a second launch must exit and open the running one; the tray menu's Quit must end the app. M2 runs with the pretend panel too · a hidden window with no panel stranded the app, reachable only by kill · yes

M2(c): the results list must draw its first row (`KEEPER RESULTS: q= shown= first=`) with as many rows as sqlite finds and a first path among them · the printed at= and a screenshot's contrast were the only proofs of searchability and of shown results · yes
- D-1622 · Keeper sends one notification per burst of alerts (sent when none came for 0.5 s, or 2 s after the first; it names the file, or "<n> files mention <word>" and the first 50 names) and none for a file saved again with the same text (a hash per path); a reindex that fails puts its changes back at the head of the queue, shows and prints the error, and tries again a second later; a partial or failed watcher is shown as the problem line · 20 alert files meant 20 notifications, a re-save repeated one, and a failed job lost its batch · yes
- D-1623 · (review round 1; checker tightening) M0 stamps the moment each change's last call begins (just before `close()` after a flushed write; the rename or remove itself) and FAILs an event stamped before it; events stamped inside that call (an inotify event is made during close(), before it returns) are counted and shown, not hidden by the clamp; the lag from that call's start (never below the real lag) must have p95 ≤ 50 ms too; the ms= allowance drops from 10 events to 3 (still 2 ms each: the test's estimate rides the file system's coarse ctime clock; the review run had 0 over, worst 1.67 ms) · the clamp to 0 hid early stamps and the allowance was looser than the data needed · yes
- D-1624 · (review round 1; checker additions) M3 requires a `web_push` try (with evidence) for run_closed and notify_closed, a parallel read among the folder reads, and recomputes the race numbers from web.json's raw stamps (each read's page-clock t0/t1; each change's [closed, seen] and the clock offset): the race's web_ms must be the fastest read and the best p95 as recomputed · the checker trusted web_ms as written, and the read it raced was a strawman (one file at a time) · yes
- D-1625 · (review round 1; checker tightening) tests/test_watch.mojo also prints `got=<ns>`, when its own (the consumer's) thread took the event from the pipe; M0 FAILs a missing or earlier got=, and the race against the web uses the later of the two p95s (reader stamp, consumer stamp), since the web's lag is stamped in the page's callback, after delivery. The workloads still differ (M0: 1,000 mixed changes 2 ms apart; the web: 200 creates 20 ms apart in a new subfolder), said where the numbers are shown · the native lag was stamped on the reader thread, before any delivery · yes
- D-1626 · (review round 1) the web's FileSystemObserver lag is measured in three rounds of 200 changes, and the median of the three p95s races (every round's raw stamps kept, recomputed by the checker; the race still takes the better of that median and polling's best p95) · one round is one sample: the first run's single round gave p95 1.84 ms, the next 0.28 ms, both on a machine with load ~9-11 from a parallel goal; racing one lucky or unlucky round decides a sub-millisecond race by noise · yes

## Probe fixes (2026-10-30, owner: fix the probes' open items, one at a time)
- D-1700 · `Microphone.captured_ns` retries an odd sequence word for at most 2 ms (STAMP_WAIT_NS; the writer's update is three stores), then returns the unpaired time clamped to now (off by one 20 ms chunk at most) · a capture thread that died or was descheduled mid-update hung the UI thread forever. A time bound, not a count: a count's length depends on the CPU. Test: tests/test_audio_stamp.mojo (a hand-made block whose word stays odd; before: the test hung) · yes
- D-1701 · the GTK test host gains `waitfor:Title=N` (run the main loop until a button titled Title exists, N ms at most; `WAITED: Title after N ms`, else `TEST: no button`), and goal 30's M1 and M3 wait for the import sheet's Create table (60 s at most) instead of a fixed 3.5 s · the flake's cause, reproduced: the 100M-row open lands in 1.8–2.3 s on an idle machine and 4.3–6.0 s with half the CPU busy, and the old steps failed every time at that load ("no button Create table"). Goal 30's contract names no time for opening; M1's frame gaps now cover the whole open, not its first 3.5 s (a tightening). Tried and dropped: lowering a job thread's priority (setpriority, nice 10): the engine's ~48 pool threads don't inherit it and examples/jobwake showed no gain under load (8 runs each); asked of the engine (mxframe-needs #27) · yes
- D-1702 · the GTK test host's `pause:` turns the main loop with `pump()` (sleeps 1 ms only when a turn did nothing) instead of sleeping 10 ms after every turn; under MOJOUI_TRACE a skipped frame prints `TRACE: frame gap N ms at T ms, paint P ms` · sampling the UI thread from /proc showed it in hrtimer_nanosleep for ~30 ms inside goal 30 M1's cancel gap; several sources are due each frame, so a busy window skipped frames. The cancel's longest gap was 50–67 ms in 6 of 6 runs, 16.7–50 after; goal 30 M1 passed 5 of 5 and then 3 of 3 (scripts/probe_fixes.py jot_flake). What's left: an occasional 50 ms gap (3 frames, at the check's limit) when the shared machine is loaded (load average 14–18 from other sessions) · yes
- D-1703 · a GTK field change that reaches update() directly drops the field's held changes (drop_held) · a held change (D-1321) then a newer one sent directly left the older waiting, and the next rebuild's drain wrote it back over the newer text. No natural path to the window was found by reading (every entry point checks busy()), so the test makes it directly: examples/fieldcheck's FIELDCHECK_FLAT mode (typing doesn't rebuild; a Rebuild button does) and the GTK test step `holdtext:Field=text` (a held change, as if it came during a rebuild); tests/test_field_queue.py red before (the app's texts newer, newer, old, old), green after. A submit doesn't drop: a held text equal to the field's must still reach the app as a change · yes
- D-1704 · the web host's files: a drop or upload checks `closed` after writing and removes what it wrote (and stops writing once evicted); every upload/download folder names its server's pid in `.owner`, and a starting server sweeps folders whose owner is gone (and unowned ones untouched for a day: older servers wrote none); a file over OMXCODE_UPLOAD_MAX keeps nothing and the page gets a host notice (toast look, gone after 8 s) · tests/test_web_files.py, red before on each: the evicted drop left small.md in a recreated folder, an oversize drop answered nothing, the sweep left the gone server's folder, and an upload evicted mid-write called the closed app and crashed the server (exit 139, seen with the new check removed). A pid can be reused, so a gone server's folder may outlive it until the next start; the day-old rule for unowned folders could sweep a live old-version server's untouched session (not the case for any server built from this host) · yes
- D-1705 · `mojoui.watch.file_stamp` reads `statx` on Linux (AT_FDCWD, STATX_BASIC_STATS; stx_size at 40, stx_mtime at 112, a 32-bit nsec) instead of `stat` at x86-64's offsets; the Mac keeps stat's 64-bit-inode layout (arm64). Every C call in watch.mojo that takes a path keeps the string alive past the call · tests/test_file_stamp.py compares with os.stat (a nanosecond time, a 5 GiB sparse file, an empty file, a missing one) and compiles a probe for aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu. Red before: two of the three real files read (0, -1): the path was freed before stat read it (the lessons' first rule). statx's struct is the kernel's uapi layout on every architecture, and needs glibc 2.28 (older glibc exports no `stat` symbol either). Unverified: running on aarch64 (no machine, sysroot or emulator image here) · yes
- D-1706 · `App.one_instance()`, a static method ("" by default): a name for an app that runs as one instance; the GTK host registers `dev.mojoui.bg.<name>` on the session bus before anything is built and, when the app is already running, sends activate and exits (no App, no shared data, no GPU probe). Background mode no longer implies one instance by itself: Keeper and traycheck declare it (same ids as before: keeper, traycheck) · D-1619 derived the id from title() and background(), instance methods, so the App had to exist first (Keeper opened its index and started a watcher in the second process). Test: goal 45 M1 gains an addition (the second launch must not print traycheck's `TRAYCHECK: built`), red before, PASS after; goal 45 M2 PASS; Keeper's Mac build links. Elsewhere (AppKit, the web) each launch is still its own · yes
- D-1707 · a layer that only lost ops keeps its other ops' render nodes: `Scene.removed_ops(i, other, j)` says which went (`removes()` now wraps it with `removed_box`), and the GTK host makes the new layer node from the old node's children minus those (`trimmed_node`; only when every op is one child: polygons, rects, circles, lines, images, fills; otherwise built as before) and mends the texture with only the children near the erased box (`nodes_near`). framebench reports `trimmed_by_layer` · measured first: an erase frame here was 21.4 ms p95, draw 20.2 = the layer node built again from 1,000 polygons (6.1 ms median) + the mend rendering the whole layer node clipped to the box (11.1) + finding the removal (1.2). After: 8.0 ms p95 (five runs 5.8, 6.7, 15.4, 8.2, 8.0 on a shared machine, load 4–16), ink built 0 and trimmed 38 times; goal 40 M3's pixels equal the CPU render (0 off) in both sessions. Goal 40 M4 gains an addition: erase p95 ≤ 16.7 ms and no ink rebuild while erasing. Generic: any canvas layer that loses ops (a chart's removed series, a deleted shape) takes the same path · yes
- D-1707 (addendum) · the bench at the final commit (cca4cd7, after the review's waitfor change) gives erase p95 8.5 ms (ink trimmed 38, built 0); the docs quote that file: 8.5 ms, six runs 5.8–15.4 · yes

Before this, a chunk without an id was refused and a tool call sent as chunks was never marked done, so a render_ui sent that way would never have drawn. REASONING_* become "reasoning" messages that go back with the next run (RunAgentInput accepts them; Pydantic AI reads them). AgentChat shows "Thinking…" over the newest words, then a folded "Thought" built from a new framework Disclosure (mojoui.disclosure), which genui's catalog can use later. Subagent lanes are not kept apart (one open chunk), and REASONING_ENCRYPTED_VALUE is not sent back yet · reversible: yes

- D-5310 · (owner, 2026-11-05: "the lowest common denominator should not exist… it's not only the best way to write it, it's also the best performance") a GPU picture stays on the GPU by default wherever a host can take one. `Gpu.draw[f](surface, p)` runs a plain pixel function into a Canvas at its real size: on GTK with NVIDIA the picture is handed to mojoui.present (GPU→GPU into a GL texture, no copy to main memory, no wait); elsewhere it's copied into the canvas; with no GPU the same function runs on the CPU (tests/test_gpu_draw.mojo: CPU, the copy and the GL texture equal byte for byte at 333×197 and 1920×1080, the buffer reused going back down, a 0×0 canvas skipped; the error path, which drops to the CPU, isn't forced by a test). Every app with a GPU canvas declares `GPU_CANVAS` (examples/gpu, lens, particles); apps without one keep it False, so they start no CUDA and keep GTK's own renderer (D-752 stands for them). examples/gpu moves from `gpu.render` + `Image` to a `Canvas` drawn with `Gpu.draw` (`gpu.render` stays, for its CPU check). Proven on the desktop (Wayland, RTX 3090): gpu_copied 3 / cpu_copied 0 for examples/gpu, 1 / 0 for lens. Next: the Mac (Apple GPU through MAX's Metal, the picture in an IOSurface), then the web (spike-webgpu) · reversible? yes
- D-5311 · the Mac keeps a GPU picture on the GPU too, with no copy at all: Apple silicon's CPU and GPU share one memory, so the host offers each Canvas draw an IOSurface (three per canvas, the first Core Animation isn't showing; whole 16 KB pages, since Metal only lends memory in pages), `Gpu.draw` wraps it for the GPU (`DeviceContext.wrap_host_memory`) and writes the picture into it as B, G, R, A, and the host shows that IOSurface as a sublayer's contents. Needs the native Mac build (scripts/build.sh with MAC_GPU on a Mac: Mojo's runtime and MAX's Metal path; it now passes -D MOJOUI_MAC_GPU); the cross build keeps the CPU path. Only pixel functions run on the Mac's GPU (PIXEL_GPU); point clouds and tables keep to GPU_BUILD until checked on Metal. A canvas that doesn't draw into its IOSurface the first time is never offered one again (omxplorer's CPU canvases hold no extra memory). Pixel kernels take Params as four scalars, rebuilt inside: Metal fails to compile a kernel that reads a SIMD argument ("Failed to create compute pipeline state", ci/mac/pixelprobe.mojo on GitHub's macos-26 runner; MAX 26.7). Proven on that runner's virtual GPU ("Apple Paravirtual device"): "GPU canvas pictures go to the screen without a copy", the canvas's report says `iosurface 3`, and the GPU's picture matches the CPU's. Then on a real Apple M4 (the owner's Mac, over SSH; the app GitHub's macos-26 job built natively, gpu-mac-gpu.zip): "GPU: Apple M4", the same no-copy line, `iosurface 1/2/3` as the picture changed, Check on CPU ✓ matches · reversible? yes

The wait sits on a server worker (counted with MAX_WAITING), tick answers it; the console's load calls wait up to 11 minutes instead of its 15 s. 55-check M3 now loads the model cold first and wants ready. A load for a model already running answers at once, before the MAX_WAITING cap; a 503 for a server that exited carries its exit and log tail (supervisor.last_error); a daemon that stops answers a waiting load 503 "stopping" instead of dropping it. Found with it: the supervisor's idle reaper counted from the start, so a model slower to start than its keep_alive would be unloaded on its first ready sweep; a child now counts as used when it turns ready. ## Goal 58: video and pictures on the Mac without ffmpeg
- D-5801 · on a Mac, mojoui.video decodes through AVFoundation (AVAssetReader → BGRA pixel buffers → RGBA), stills through ImageIO (CGImageSource → a CGBitmapContext, un-premultiplied), and mojoui.videoexport encodes through AVAssetWriter (H.264, .mp4): mojoui.avmedia, behind VideoSource's and VideoWriter's own APIs (comptime if CompilationTarget.is_macos(); Linux keeps ffmpeg, the reference), over bundling ffmpeg (licences, size) or a libav binding (a dependency); the frameworks are dlopen'd and called through the Objective-C runtime and dlsym, so the Mac build links nothing new · reversible
- D-5802 · structs over 16 bytes (CMTime 24, CMTimeRange 48) go to Objective-C as a pointer to a copy (arm64's rule for composites that aren't HFAs); a CMTime comes back through NSValue getValue:size: (the asset's duration), never as a return value; the frame count is duration × fps, rounded (AVFoundation has no nb_frames) · reversible
- D-5803 · a Mac seek starts AVAssetReader a quarter frame into frame n: it starts at the frame whose span holds the start time, where ffmpeg's -ss drops frames before it (VideoSource asks half a frame early there); half a frame early gave n − 1 on the Mac (frames 12 and 100 differed), a quarter frame in gives Linux's frame within 2 levels · reversible
- D-5804 · the host's wake from a worker thread is mojoui.wake.UIWaker (the pipe on Linux; on a Mac jobsEnded: through dispatch_async_f onto the main queue), used by mojoui.jobs.WakeUI and mojoui.framefeed: a FrameFeed only wrote the pipe, which AppKit doesn't watch, so a video's probe never reached a Mac's screen; and performSelectorOnMainThread:… from a raw pthread never arrived (traced on the owner's Mac), where dispatch_async_f does · reversible
- D-5805 · scripts/mac-ssh-test runs a Mojo test on the owner's Mac (VAR=value pairs with {data}/{src}); the media tests print the same lines on both platforms (64-cell thumbnails of frames and pictures) so Linux's decoder is the Mac's reference · reversible
